# Agent ledger

One row per unit of work. **Every agent working on this repo reads this file first,
claims its row, and writes its evidence when done.**

**Also read the live truth surface before acting:**
`https://www.galaxysportsedge.com/api/ops/public-surface-truth` — deployment SHA,
gates, credit stack, Jynx lane plan, calibration. On 2026-08-19 it disproved a
"leaked Anthropic key" alarm (the value was Vercel placeholder text; the real
value is `auto`) and would have prevented a week-old open PR (#369) from being
rebuilt twice by different agents. Dashboards and prose reports drift; this
endpoint and this ledger are the two ground truths.

Validated by `scripts/ops/check-agent-ledger.mjs`, which runs in CI via
`apps/web/__tests__/agent-ledger.test.ts`. A malformed or dishonest row fails the
build.

## Why this exists

Four agents work on `Beexly/Sports` — a local Hermes runner, GitHub Copilot, a
browser agent, and Claude Code sessions. They share no memory. On 2026-08-19 that
cost three separate collisions in one day:

- Copilot and Hermes each built the same fabricated `signup-workflow` feature,
  independently, neither knowing the other had.
- A Claude session began reimplementing `H-L` while it was assigned to Hermes.
- Hermes read "run the queue" as a CSV import queue and processed a census file
  instead of the task queue.

Git is the only substrate all four agents actually touch — none of them can reach a
Postgres or Redis memory store. So the ledger is a file, and the enforcement is a
guard, not a convention.

Related prior art, and why it is not used: `Beexly/fablechain` carries an
`AgentMemory` (Postgres + Redis) and a `TaskBacklog`. Over its last 200 commits the
agent implemented `feat(faucet): implement testnet faucet backend` **nine times** with
an identical subject line, because `TaskBacklog.ts` is a `const` array and completing
a task never removes it. Persistent memory alone does not prevent duplicate work.
Evidence does.

## Rules

1. **Claim before you start.** Set `Owner` and `Status: CLAIMED` in the same commit
   that begins the work. An unclaimed row is fair game; a claimed row is not yours.
2. **Never edit a row you do not own**, except to add a `BLOCKED` note explaining a
   dependency.
3. **`DONE` requires evidence.** Put a commit SHA (7+ hex chars) or a `#123` PR
   reference in `Evidence`. The guard resolves SHAs against the repo — a hash that
   does not exist fails the build. "I completed it" is not evidence.
4. **If you cannot push, you are `UNPUSHED`, not `DONE`.** Record the local branch
   and SHA so the work can be recovered. Eight rows sit in this state right now;
   every one of them exists on exactly one laptop.
5. **`CANCELLED` requires a reason** in `Evidence`, not a hash.
6. **Titles must be unique.** Two rows describing the same work is the exact failure
   this file exists to catch, so the guard rejects it.
7. **One row, one unit of work.** If scope grows, add a row; do not widen a claimed one.

## Owners

| Owner | What it is |
|---|---|
| `hermes` | Local Hermes runner (OpenRouter free models), on the founder's machine |
| `copilot` | GitHub Copilot coding agent, runs on a GitHub runner |
| `browser` | Browser agent with dashboard access (Vercel, Anthropic console) |
| `claude` | Claude Code session (remote container) |
| `founder` | Garrett — owner-gated decisions only |
| `—` | Unclaimed |

## Statuses

`OPEN` · `CLAIMED` · `BLOCKED` · `UNPUSHED` · `DONE` · `CANCELLED`

`UNPUSHED` is the honest state for work that is finished but lives only on one
machine. Hermes is instructed never to push, so its deliverables sit on a local
branch whose SHAs no other clone can resolve. That is not `DONE`: one laptop is
the only copy, and nobody else can verify or build on it.

WARNING (7d425f47): do NOT implement the grouping-loss formula supplied by the external adversary — it gives within-bin OUTCOME variance, which for binary outcomes is ~p(1-p), near 0.25 always, and is useless. True grouping loss is the variance of the TRUE conditional probability within a score bin (expected within-bin variance of P(Y=1 given X), conditioned on the score S(X)) and requires the paper's partition/clustering lower-bound estimator over features within each bin. Its accompanying <0.01 / 0.01-0.05 / >0.05 threshold table is invented, not sourced. | C-113 | CRITICAL, FIXED: the free score persister settled games that had not started. apps/web/lib/data-sources/free-score-persist.ts filtered candidate finals by a +/-48h date window ("~2 days (TZ edge)") and then matched on TEAM NAMES ONLY, so in a daily series (MLB plays the same matchup on consecutive days) an earlier meeting's final was written onto a LATER unplayed game and the published picks on it were graded WIN/LOSS before first pitch. Nothing downstream caught it: SCORE_MISMATCH_CROSS_PATH only refuses to overwrite an EXISTING final with a DIFFERENT one, and a pick graded off a phantom score is not overdue, so settlement health read HEALTHY throughout. TWO PATHS, not one (CodeRabbit, #717): the score persister writes the phantom FINAL onto the Game row, but the grading that reaches a subscriber happens in apps/web/lib/data-sources/free-settlement-runner.ts via settlePendingPicks, which loaded PENDING picks with no commenceTime filter and graded them straight off a feed final with no kickoff binding. Either path alone reproduces the incident, so remediation that touches only the persister is incomplete. SHIPPED FIX, in both (the first revision ordered candidates by calendar-date distance; that was too weak and is NOT the live rule): (1) skip any game whose commenceTime is still in the future, and scope the runner's pending-pick load the same way, each re-checked in the WHERE of its own write so a game postponed mid-cycle cannot be stamped; (2) collect EVERY windowed final naming both teams, narrow with nearestByKickoff (shared by both paths), and refuse when more than one survives; (3) reject a final whose startIso drifts past MAX_KICKOFF_DRIFT_MS (12h), bind a final that carries no start time to ONE day rather than two so the UTC-rollover case still works, and refuse outright when a supplied timestamp is malformed or date-only; (4) a doubleheader is resolved by the clock, not by a flag or a count: assign both the final and the pick to their nearest board fixture and require the same one, holding when nothing places (the earlier "more fixtures than finals" count and the board's completed flag were both wrong and are NOT the live rule); (5) the settlement write is transactional and rolls back when the game refuses the FINAL write, so a concurrent postponement or a competing cross-path score can never leave a pick settled against a row the database did not stamp | claude | DONE | DONE cc161128a for the persister half; the complete guard set across both paths is #717 (branch claude/sports-launch-round2-fixes-hk7kv9). MEASURED ON PRODUCTION 2026-09-06 19:20-19:45 UTC (read-only SQL, SELECT only): 87 published picks had settledAt EARLIER than their game's commenceTime (all 87 isPublished), 63 of them MONEYLINE graded WIN/LOSS (34 WIN / 29 LOSS) and ALL 63 inside the calibration window; 5 games with a FUTURE commenceTime carried status FINAL with scores, every one updatedAt 2026-09-06T19:22:22Z (written while the audit ran, so the loop was live). Worst case pick cmtp2tipl00lf142ew8g32u0w, MONEYLINE "Philadelphia Phillies ML (model signal)", v5.2.7, result WIN, settledAt 2026-09-06T02:07:17.186Z on a game with commenceTime 2026-09-07T17:06:00Z, graded 39.0h BEFORE kickoff, game row FINAL 4-2; a logged-out visitor saw a WIN badge on an unplayed game. Root cause was the +/-48h window plus team-name-only matching in persistFreeScores' per-game loop. Tests free-score-persist.test.ts 23 passed across 5 suites at the branch tip (the row previously said 19 across 4, which was true when written and went stale as later commits added cases; CodeRabbit, #717); every guard proven by negative control, each disabling failing exactly its own test; typecheck 0, lint 0, guardrails 26/26. Found by the blind-spot sweep surface lens and independently verified before any code was touched |
| C-114 | FOUNDER DECISION then owner-run: remediate the 87 already-corrupted picks and 5 games from C-113. The C-113 guard (cc161128a) stops NEW corruption but changes nothing already written: those rows still carry fabricated WIN/LOSS results and phantom FINAL scores, and the 63 moneyline rows are inside the sample the published ECE 0.0524 is computed from, so the calibration figure is measured partly on outcomes nobody observed. THE POLICY IS THE FOUNDER'S CALL, not an agent's: revert the affected picks to PENDING and let the normal lane re-grade them after the real game finishes, or VOID them with an RCA code through the settlement outbox, or unpublish. Whichever is chosen, the game rows must also have status and scores cleared where commenceTime is still in the future. METHOD TRAP, recorded because I fell into it for one round: a side x stored x expected breakdown LOOKS directional (OVER-stored-LOSS showed +4.03, UNDER-stored-WIN +5.92) but every one of those cells is directional BY CONSTRUCTION - if OVER is stored LOSS and expected WIN then actual > line necessarily. Those cells cannot evidence direction; only the unconditional split can, and it is symmetric. WHERE IT POINTS: symmetric errors concentrated in the settle window are the signature of grading against a WRONG FIXTURE's score, which is directionally random where a partial score is not. That makes C-115 a candidate SIBLING of C-113, the cross-fixture matching defect fixed forward in #717, on the TOTAL market rather than the score persister. NEXT: test whether each contradicting pick's stored result is explained by the final of some OTHER game in the same feed window, not merely a duplicate row of the same series - hypothesis 2 tested duplicate ROWS only (10 of 80) and cross-fixture is a wider set. NO remediation shipped: the 81 rows stay published until the cause is known, because unpublishing them now destroys the evidence the next investigation needs. With that many games in a window almost every line has a neighbour on both sides, so existence carries zero information. Do not re-run it. HYPOTHESIS 7 (cross-fixture confusion on a NAME-SIMILAR fixture, the C-113 containment signature) is NOT SUPPORTED, and that is a CONFOUND rather than a clean rejection - stated that way on purpose. Restricting the neighbour to one whose team name contains or is contained by this game's, the explains-rate is 5 of 74 (6.8pct) for contradicting picks against 278 of 411 (67.6pct) for correct ones, with a similar base rate of having such a neighbour at all (91.4pct vs 81.1pct). That LOOKS decisive and is not: a name-containing neighbour inside 24h is usually a DUPLICATE ROW of the same fixture carrying the same score, so it lands on the own-game side BY CONSTRUCTION - which explains the stored result exactly when the pick is correct and never when it contradicts. Same by-construction trap as the side x stored x expected breakdown. The neighbour set has to exclude same-pairing rows before this test can say anything, and even then existence is near 50/50 under the null. NEXT: trace population B (47 rows) only - population A is measurement, not mis-grading, and is now C-143. ROOT CAUSE FOUND, AND IT INVERTS THE FINDING. I HAD THE BLAME BACKWARDS AND SO DID THIS ROW: the picks are RIGHT and the GAME ROWS are wrong. A nine-lens parallel investigation converged from six independent angles on one mechanism - the pick is graded correctly against a fetched ESPN final while its game row is still unscored, and homeScore/awayScore are written LATER by a loose team-name plus time-window backfill that stamps a DIFFERENT fixture of the same series onto the row. I then verified it MYSELF against ESPN ground truth, independent of the agents, three of three: event 401816824 really finished Mariners 2 - Athletics 6 (total 8) but our row says 6-7 (13); event 401816839 really finished 2 - 0 (total 2), our row says 6-7 (13); event 401816841 really finished Dodgers 7 - Nationals 5 (total 12), our row says 5-3 (total 8). In ALL THREE the stored pick result is CORRECT against the true final. cmtp7m2g1 OVER 6.6 stored LOSS is right (true total 8 against clvLockLine 8.375); cmtpi1g74 OVER 7.0 stored LOSS is right (true total 2); cmtp7m2zq UNDER 8.5 stored LOSS is right (true total 12). The 2026-09-05 Mariners game's 6-7 was copied onto two LATER, DISTINCT fixtures of the same series, written at 04:17:41 and 22:47:30 - after those picks settled. THIS EXPLAINS EVERY SIGNAL AT ONCE, which no earlier hypothesis did: the symmetry (a wrong fixture's score is directionally random), the market-agnosticism (a wrong score corrupts TOTAL, SPREAD and MONEYLINE alike, which is why the moneyline column was the discriminating fact), the 13x timing split (a pick settled soon after kickoff has an unscored row, so the loose backfill is what fills it and that is where the mis-bind happens) and the identical scoreline across two commenceTimes I flagged and did not chase. CONSEQUENCE, and it is the important one: REMEDIATION BY UNPUBLISHING THESE PICKS WOULD DESTROY CORRECT RESULTS. The public track record may be healthier than reported; the SCORES shown beside it are what is wrong. Also visible in the same query and not incidental: each fixture exists as THREE game rows under different externalId namespaces (espn:mlb:401816795, espn:baseball_mlb:401816795, and a hash) - C-85 and C-117 are live and are the surface the loose backfill mis-binds across. NEXT: fix the score-persist bind, not the picks; re-derive the 117 against ESPN rather than against our own rows; NOTHING gets unpublished on the strength of the old reading. ADVERSARIAL VERIFICATION OF THE AGENT HYPOTHESES HAD NOT COMPLETED when this was written - the ESPN checks are mine and are what this claim rests on. CAUSAL CHAIN COMPLETE 2026-09-07 21:3x UTC, and the missing capability is named. (1) A score is written WRONG - either a phantom pre-kickoff write (the C-114 population, kickoff times correct, scores wrong on 4 of 4 checked) or a cross-fixture bind carrying another game of the same series (the C-115 population, 3 of 3 confirmed against ESPN). (2) SCORE_MISMATCH_CROSS_PATH then REFUSES to overwrite an existing final with a different one - a guard that exists for a good reason, to stop one lane clobbering another's result. (3) THEREFORE THE WRONG SCORE IS PERMANENT. Measured: all six verified-wrong game rows are FROZEN, untouched for between 16h and 1d17h (espn 401816824 last written 2026-09-06 04:17 and not touched since) while settle and refresh cycles ran several times an hour throughout. Nothing in the system can correct a game score once it is wrong. (4) The public surface therefore shows a wrong score beside a correct pick, indefinitely. THE GOOD NEWS, measured not assumed: the CURRENT settlement path is healthy. C-120's settledWith payload is live as of 2026-09-07 20:20 UTC and on all 14 events recorded so far the score the grade was computed from matches the game row EXACTLY, written within a fraction of a second of each other - so the lane running now writes score and grade atomically from one payload and is not producing new corruption. free-score-persist.ts is also already well guarded (doubleheader placement, nearestByKickoff, fail-closed on ties, MAX_KICKOFF_DRIFT_MS); those guards are NOT the gap. THE GAP IS THAT NO AUTHENTICATED CORRECTION PATH EXISTS. The cross-path guard is right to refuse an unauthenticated overwrite; what is missing is a lane that may overwrite a final WHEN AND ONLY WHEN a named ground-truth source disagrees with it, writing an audit row for every correction. That is the remediation for C-115 and it is also what makes the settled-before-kickoff population re-gradeable instead of deletable. It is a new write path over game scores, so it is FOUNDER-GATED, not an agent decision. NOTHING further should be unpublished. CORRECTION TO MY OWN CORRECTION, from adversarial verification 2026-09-07 21:5x UTC, and I confirmed the counter-evidence myself before accepting it. I wrote "zero mis-graded pick results; only the reference is corrupt". THAT IS TOO STRONG. GENUINELY MIS-GRADED PUBLISHED PICKS EXIST: cmtp1qor6052dx2r51abbm3ik, published MONEYLINE "Seattle Mariners ML", v5.2.7, on espn:mlb:401816839, settled 2026-09-06T02:07:17Z against a 20:12:24Z first pitch = 18.09 HOURS BEFORE the game, stored LOSS; ESPN has Mariners 2-0 winners, so the true result is WIN and the stored LOSS is exactly what the row's corrupt 6-7 implies. THE MODEL THAT FITS BOTH: two failure modes separated by WHEN the pick was graded. Graded AFTER kickoff from a fetched payload, the pick is correct and the row is corrupted later (the C-115 population). Graded BEFORE kickoff there is nothing to fetch, so it is graded off whatever the row already held, INHERITS the corruption, and is right or wrong by coincidence (the C-114 population, which is why 3 of 4 measured correct - a coin flip against a wrong score). The populations OVERLAP; that moneyline is in both. WHAT THE VERIFIER CONFIRMED, harder than I did: it tested by ESPN EVENT ID rather than team-plus-time - the field a duplicate-fixture defect corrupts - and rebuilt the population independently (605/88/37/51 against my 588/81/34/47, same structure, live drift), verifying 45 of 45. WHAT IT REFUTED: my claim that the corrupt-score story explains the 13x timing split. It ran the control I did not - corruption in the CORRECT cohort is 39.6pct under 5h against 50.0pct at 5h+, flat to inverted - so a corrupt baseline cannot produce the population-B gradient. THE TIMING SIGNAL REMAINS UNEXPLAINED. Also refuted: the paid lane as writer (settle-sport.ts:552 gates the whole grading block on !conflicts, so that path is unreachable); the writer is re-attributed to the stale backfill lane, which is AGENT-REPORTED and NOT yet confirmed by me. |

The workflow STALLED before its refutation phase, so NONE of these carry adversarial verification and they must not be treated as confirmed. CONFIRMED AND ALREADY FIXED: the postponed/cancelled VOID path bypassed every guard this PR added, voiding a live game off a sibling fixture's postponement - fixed in b8c05c7c2, and independently reported by Devin Review the same hour, which is the strongest quality signal the audit produced. REFUTED, do not act on it as written: the claim that settle-sport.ts:474 has 'no score predicate' is wrong - it has a SCORE_MISMATCH_CROSS_PATH conflict check that refuses the overwrite. What IS true there is narrower: the check is a preceding READ and the write is an unguarded update by id with no commenceTime bound, so it carries the same read-then-write race shape the runner just closed. SINCE VERIFIED AND FIXED (second confirmed finding): the zero-sit VOID write carried no kickoff predicate. I have now read the lane's selection logic and it is NOT a founder policy question as I first recorded it - decideZeroSitVoid returns skip for every candidate under the minimum age, so ageHours >= the floor is a NECESSARY condition of every void this lane issues, and that age is computed from the commenceTime captured at candidate load. The scoreboard fetch for up to 20 picks sits between that read and the write, so the window is wide. The unpublish half of the SAME FILE already carries its full predicate into its own updateMany with a comment giving exactly this reason, so this is an internal inconsistency, not a design choice. The game-cancel write had the same hole and was worse: a game rescheduled into the future and still SCHEDULED would be marked CANCELED. Both bounds now ride in the WRITE as relation filters; a refused write lands in the existing WRITE_RACE_LOST bucket and the pick stays PENDING for the next cycle, so no new accounting was needed and no VOID semantics changed. ALSO VERIFIED AND FIXED (third confirmed finding): settle-backfill graded the pick FIRST, then on a score mismatch declined the game write and let the grade stand - the code said so outright, 'the pick settlement already happened above'. That commits a grade computed from the incoming score onto a game row that keeps a different one, and records it nowhere. This produces exactly the signature I measured on production in C-115, settled picks contradicting the score on their own row; state it as a MECHANISM that produces that signature, NOT as the established cause of those 80 rows, which I have not traced to this path. Fixed by rolling the whole transaction back through a sentinel throw: the grade rests on the same contested number as the score, so both go or neither does. The refusal is now recorded as held with reason SCORE_MISMATCH, a new member of the UnresolvedStalePick reason union with no consumer outside this file and its tests, so the pick does not sit unaccounted and the zero-sit lane takes it under SCORE_MISMATCH_CROSS_PATH. The preceding read plus unguarded update by id was ALSO the read-then-write race shape, so the rule now rides in the write as a guarded updateMany, with the read kept only for the log line on the refusal path. FOURTH CONFIRMED FINDING, VERIFIED AND FIXED: the AMBIGUOUS_MATCH hold reporting an empty sources array. Confirmed, and MORE SPECIFIC than the lens stated. There are THREE AMBIGUOUS_MATCH holds in free-settlement.ts, not two: the city-only hold (empty sources), the own-fixture hold added by THIS PR's own fixture-binding work (also empty sources), and the disagreeing-candidates hold (sources populated). The zero-sit lane derived the cause from sources.length === 0, so the own-fixture hold was reported to operators as a city-only team NAME when the names were fine and the fixture simply could not be placed by any clock. Both causes void under the same rcaCode so no ACTION was wrong; the RCA reason an operator reads to diagnose the void was. On this product a false statement in an audit record is not cosmetic. Fixed by having the grader STATE its cause on an optional ambiguity field at all three holds, with the old inference kept as the fallback for an outcome predating the field, a third ZeroSitAmbiguityCause member NO_OWN_FIXTURE with its own detail text, and the own-fixture hold now also carrying the sources of the finals it could not place. BOTH REMAINING FINDINGS REFUTED AS ACTIONABLE, do not act on them as written. (a) 'finalBelongsToOwnFixture's rows.length < 2 is evaluated AFTER filters that can drop the sibling fixture': mechanically true, but those filters are LOAD-BEARING and removing them is a regression, not a fix. sameMatchupFixturesNearKickoff is called with a MULTI-DAY board, so the same two teams appear on it for every game of a series, and the startTime and drift filters are exactly what separates a doubleheader sibling from tomorrow's series game. Keeping an undatable row would make an ordinary series look like a doubleheader and hold, then VOID, every pick in it. free-settlement.test.ts already pins this - 'still settles when the only other board row is an adjacent-day clockless fixture' is a CodeRabbit regression guard from earlier in this same PR - so acting on this finding would have re-broken a bug already fixed here. The rows those filters drop that a person would call a sibling are ones with no usable date, and those cannot be placed by clock anyway, which is the same reason the guard refuses when it does see them. (b) 'the tie detector may treat two distinct fixtures listed at the same instant as one': the s !== best clause does tolerate one instant appearing twice, but it is load-bearing too. multi-source-scores.ts mergeGames dedupes on each source's OWN gameId, so one fixture reported by two sources with different ids survives as two rows at the identical instant; removing the clause would make those tie and refuse, holding and then voiding gradable picks. That case and a genuine same-instant doubleheader are NOT distinguishable with the data finalMatchesNearestFixture receives (start instants only, no fixture identity), and not by any fixture-identity key either, since two distinct fixtures with the same teams at the same instant collapse under any such key. A documented limitation, not a defect with a fix. NOTED, low: the scoreless hold's no-op update still bumps Game.updatedAt, a Prisma @updatedAt column, so 'it changes nothing' is true of the VALUE and false of the ROW | claude | OPEN | Audit run wf_a52fe63b-3a1, 8 lenses over free-settlement.ts, free-score-persist.ts, free-settlement-runner.ts, settle-backfill.ts, zero-sit-lane.ts and settle-sport.ts. Two lenses completed and returned 14 findings; the run stalled before the 3-skeptic refutation phase, so severities are the lens's own and unvalidated. | C-122 | Review round 18 on #717: one bug in code THIS SESSION wrote, plus two real CodeRabbit findings, one of which had an INCOMPLETE proposed fix. (1) Devin, CONFIRMED: persistZeroSitVoid guarded the pick write with a relation filter on game.commenceTime, but a relation filter locks the PICK row, not the game. A reschedule committing between the pick write and the cancellation left the transaction committing a permanent VOID on a game that will be played, with the cancel matching nothing. My own comment said Prisma does not lock the game row and I then relied on a filter that only reads it - the exact 'fixed the branch I was looking at' pattern the check-in warns about. Fixed by acquiring the game row LOCK FIRST: write the kickoff back to the value the candidate carried, matched on that exact value, which refuses any move and holds the row to commit. Same lock acquisition free-settlement-runner.ts uses. (2) CodeRabbit, CONFIRMED: findPostponedMatch copied a DATE-ONLY board start into TrustedFinal.startIso, which parses as midnight UTC, so a timed pick read as more than MAX_KICKOFF_DRIFT_MS adrift and the postponement went undetected (PENDING/NO_FINAL instead of its direct VOID). IMPORTANT, their proposed fix was INCOMPLETE and adopting it verbatim REOPENED a bug this PR had already fixed: dropping startIso alone falls through to finalBindsToKickoff's clockless +/-1 DAY calendar rule, so YESTERDAY's postponement voided a game played today, which is the series bug the clock binding exists to stop arriving through the clockless path. Caught by writing the negative test alongside the positive one. Real fix is both halves: omit startIso when the row has no clock AND require a clockless postponement to be the pick's own DAY, matching how sameMatchupFixturesNearKickoff already narrows clockless fixtures. (3) CodeRabbit, CONFIRMED: persistFreeScores queried commenceTime gte only, so every FUTURE scheduled fixture for the sport matched the OR clause (all SCHEDULED with null scores) and competed for the take 300 cap with the started games the pass exists to score. Worse than reported: MLB runs about 15 games a day, so the 21-day window is already near the cap BEFORE any future fixture is counted, and the starved rows are exactly the ones that go overdue. Fixed with an upper bound plus a deterministic oldest-first ordering (the cap was previously an arbitrary database slice). (4) CodeRabbit, ACCEPTED as a records-honesty fix: the C-118 row asserted a live production bypass that 2f9f7d90e had already closed; reworded to historical with the remediation scope kept OPEN. | claude | DONE | #717. Each fix has an independent negative control: removing the game hold fails the two new ordering/refusal tests (writeOrder reads pick-first instead of game-first); restoring the date-only startIso fails the date-only VOID test; and the yesterday-date-only test is what caught the incomplete fix mid-flight. Four existing zero-sit assertions were retargeted from gameUpdates to a new gameCancels list rather than loosened, since the kickoff hold is a lock acquisition and not a cancellation, so each keeps its exact original meaning. NOTE the fake cannot model row locking, so what the tests prove is the ORDER (the hold runs first) and the REFUSAL (a moved game stops everything at the hold); the lock semantics rest on Postgres. Winner is deterministic: strongest lane (PUBLISHED over SCORING over GATED), then higher confidence, then higher edge, then later updatedAt, then lexically smallest id, so the board does not flip between loads. DELIBERATELY NOT KEYED ON THE MATCHUP STRING: matchup is built from denormalized team-name columns that differ between rows for the same fixture, and keying on names is the identity guess game-merge-plan.ts explicitly fails closed on (a bare 'Los Angeles' must never prefix-match; MLB has two LA clubs). Collapsing two genuinely different games is worse than showing one twice. ALSO: mergedIntoGameId null now filters the board's decision query and the /api/picks gameFilter. That is the database's OWN canonicity marker so it needs no guess, and gameFilter is shared by the findMany AND the totalAvailableToday count, so the free tier's published-today number cannot disagree with the rows it counts. STILL OPEN, state it plainly: two NEVER-MERGED game rows for one real contest are still two rows on the board and two picks in the API. | C-158 | REVIEW ROUND 35, Devin, CONFIRMED AND IT IS A COST OF MY OWN C-156 FIX THAT I STATED AS IF IT WERE FREE. C-156 made the slate's isPublished write ONE-DIRECTIONAL: a closed gate forces false every run, an open gate writes nothing, so an operator's withdrawal survives. Devin's point is the other half of that: GATE RECOVERY NO LONGER RESTORES. If canExposePublicPicks closes and later reopens, the rows the closed runs unpublished are never republished by the open ones, and since a pick is unique per (gameId, pickType) no new row can replace them - they stay hidden for the rest of their life. That is a real regression against the previous behaviour. My C-156 comment said 'a pick that was never published stays that way' and framed it as intent without naming the price. WHY IT IS STILL THE RIGHT DIRECTION, and this is a judgement rather than a measurement: the two failures are not symmetric. The old behaviour silently RE-PUBLISHED a pick an operator had withdrawn for being wrong or corrupt, which publishes a known falsehood. The new behaviour publishes LESS than it could. Under this product's premise the safe direction is publishing less. WHY AN AGENT CANNOT FIX IT: doing both correctly requires distinguishing 'unpublished by the gate' from 'unpublished by an operator', and `isPublished` is a bare Boolean (schema.prisma:550) with nowhere to record which. That needs a provenance column, i.e. a migration - and packages/db/prisma/schema.prisma plus migrations are FROZEN for agents by AGENTS.md law 2. So this is founder-gated by construction, not by preference. SUGGESTED SHAPE when the founder takes it: an `unpublishedReason` enum or nullable string written by whoever unpublishes (GATE_CLOSED vs OPERATOR_WITHDRAWN vs REMEDIATION), then the open-gate branch restores ONLY GATE_CLOSED rows. Both board lanes order a withdrawal against gated evaluations using the PUBLICATION's evaluatedAt because no withdrawal time exists, and a reason-only column does not fix that. THE REAL FINDING, MEASURED on settled published non-bootstrap picks with a decided result: THE PICKS YOU COULD ACTUALLY PLACE PERFORM WORST. MLB totals on a real ladder 70 of 193 = 36.3pct, off-ladder 136 of 275 = 49.5pct. MLB spreads on-ladder 127 of 294 = 43.2pct, off-ladder 116 of 232 = 50.0pct. Spreads and totals are near-even-money so break-even is about 52.4pct and win rate is approximately the profitability metric: both on-ladder cohorts are losing, and 36.3pct over 193 is roughly 3.8 sd below a coin flip, not sampling noise. TWO CONTROLS RUN: all four cohorts are isBootstrap false, so it is not a pre-canonical artifact; mean bookmakerCount is 8.32 on-ladder against 8.26 off-ladder, so the cohorts are not separated by how much data we had. THE CONFOUND, NAMED NOT BURIED: with ~8 books an on-ladder average means the books AGREED and an off-ladder one means they DISAGREED, so this contrasts market consensus as much as placeability and the two cannot be separated by this query [INFERRED]. That does not weaken the actionable part - the engine does materially worse where the market is confident, which is the opposite of where an edge should live - and the placeable subset remains the honest denominator for any public claim. ALSO NOT ESTABLISHED: these are stored results and C-150 measured 68 of 590 moneylines wrong; the corruption is not known to be balanced across these cohorts, and nobody has re-derived SPREAD or TOTAL results against ESPN because C-143 means the correct result is not yet well defined for those markets. LAUNCH WARNING that follows directly: win rate by market is NCAAF ML 94.1pct (n 102), MLB ML 62.8pct (n 608), MLB spread 46.2pct (n 526), MLB total 44.0pct (n 468). A BLENDED WIN RATE ACROSS THESE WOULD BE MISLEADING IN OUR FAVOUR - moneyline win rate is not a performance claim without price, since the engine only takes sides at fairProb >= 0.58, so a high ML win rate is what backing favourites looks like. PERFORMANCE_STATS_ENABLED is off; before it is flipped the performance surface must be checked for exactly this mixing. That is a gate, so no agent touches it. | claude | OPEN | docs/ops/PLACEABILITY_AND_PERFORMANCE_2026-09-07.md. All figures read-only SELECT via the Neon MCP; no writes; no code changed. TWO THINGS, AND THE FIRST IS A PROCESS MISS WORTH RECORDING: apps/web has tests in TWO places - __tests__/ and COLOCATED beside the source (lib/data-sources/free-score-persist.test.ts) - and I verified C-170 by running only __tests__/. The colocated suite pinned take:300 and went red in CI on 23d7648a0 and again on f90bd3ffa. The repo-wide `npm test` is the CI-equivalent command and is what I should have run; it reproduces in one shot. Assertion updated to 2000 with the reason, not deleted. SECOND, AND IT IS A REAL BUG (Devin Review, #719): the candidate window is an INSTANT interval, 21*24h back from now, and an instant interval that does not begin at midnight touches 22 DISTINCT Eastern calendar dates. uniqueScoreboardDates was called with maxDays 21 and keeps the NEWEST 21, while the candidate query orders games OLDEST first - so the oldest date's games were selected as candidates and their scoreboard date was never fetched. Those games stayed unscored until they aged out of the window entirely, which is the same overdue-pick symptom C-170 was fixing from the other end. Widening the candidate cap made that day reliably populated rather than sometimes empty, which is how the review surfaced it. Now maxDays 22, pinned by a test that asserts the ARGUMENT because this suite mocks the date helper - the honest assertion available in that file, and it says so in its own body. | claude | DONE | PR #719. | C-175 | A FIXTURE WE PUBLISHED AND THEN WITHDREW WAS BEING DESCRIBED AS ONE WE NEVER LOOKED AT (Devin Review, #719). Both board lanes have a fallback query that selects games with `picks: { none: { isPublished: true } }` and labels them with `unevaluatedPassReason` - state.ts's gated lane and passes.ts's pass list. That relation excludes only a LIVE published pick, so THE MOMENT A PICK IS WITHDRAWN its fixture matches again and the surface says the game has not been evaluated. It is the same defect chronology fixed one query higher (C-149, C-153) reappearing in the fallback, and it is the strongest way these lists can be false: we evaluated it, published it, and withdrew it. FIX: both fallback queries now exclude the fixtures in the withdrawal watermark the decision lane already computes; in state.ts that map is hoisted out of the decision branch so one computation serves both paths. WHY SILENCE AND NOT A LABEL, and this is a judgement: of the options available, 'we passed' and 'not evaluated' both assert something untrue, and a third public state ('published, then withdrawn') is customer copy nobody has approved - and could not be written accurately anyway while Pick.isPublished carries no withdrawal timestamp (C-158, C-167). THIS ALSO ANSWERS DEVIN'S OTHER OPEN THREAD, 'Superseded fixtures disappear from mixed boards', WHICH PULLS THE OPPOSITE WAY: that one asks for the superseded fixture to reappear through the fallback, which is exactly the false label this row removes. The two cannot both be satisfied by the lanes as they exist; they can only both be satisfied by a fourth display state, which is founder copy plus the C-158 column. Recorded rather than silently picking one. | claude | DONE | PR #719. | C-281 | LINE INTEGRITY, PART 1 OF 4 (remediation of C-197, which lives on claude/sports-launch-round2-fixes-hk7kv9 and is NOT edited by this branch). DOES IT STILL HAPPEN: YES, and C-197's DIAGNOSIS IS WRONG IN A WAY THAT CHANGES THE REMEDY. C-197 says the `line` field holds a MODEL-PREDICTED MARGIN. It does not. scoring.ts computes `avgSpread` (packages/prediction-engine/src/scoring.ts:411) and `avgTotal` (:685) as the ARITHMETIC MEAN OF EVERY BOOK'S QUOTED LINE, and stores that mean; 'Missouri Tigers -53.8' / -53.83333333333334 is the mean of real FCS book lines on a blowout fixture, reproduced in test from [-53.5,-54,-54,-53.5,-54,-54]. The engine output is not leaking into the price field; the consensus statistic is being presented and graded as a placeable price. The defect and its severity stand - a member cannot place -53.83, and rule 1 forbids publishing it - but the write path C-197 asks to find does not exist, so there is nothing to disconnect. MEASURED PER PATH, guard OFF (production today): SPREAD off a quoted line publishes (scoring.ts:653 `line: avgSpread`); TOTAL off a quoted line publishes (:857 `line: avgTotal`); MLB spread off the +/-1.5 ladder is ALREADY REFUSED (isPublishableSpreadLine, :431, C-119/C-125); MLB TOTAL has no ladder twin and publishes 8.833 (the sibling-lane pattern again, and TOTAL is the worse half of the finding: 369 of 599 vs 310 of 719); a line with NO bookmaker quote cannot occur because MIN_BOOKMAKERS gates both scorers on quoted rows. BUILT: isQuotedBookLine(line, quotedLines) plus enforcement in both scorers, refusing the pick when the stored mean is not a line some book on the row quoted. NOT a half-point-grid test on purpose - books quote quarter-point Asian handicaps and whole-number totals, so the grid is a proxy and the book set is the fact. The baseball ladder guard is KEPT, not replaced: it catches the case this one cannot, where every book quotes the same contaminated line. ENFORCEMENT SHIPS OFF (LINE_INTEGRITY_PUBLISH_GUARD_ENABLED, default false) AND THE FLIP IS BLOCKED TO THE FOUNDER, for two reasons recorded rather than decided: (1) turning it on changes what the engine publishes, which the dispatch brief says to BLOCK, and (2) it overturns a RECORDED decision - packages/prediction-engine/src/__tests__/baseball-run-line.test.ts:31 states in terms that a blanket must-be-quoted rule 'would gut the board' and keeps the ladder baseball-only on purpose. On C-197's own production numbers the flip suppresses ~43pct of SPREAD and ~62pct of TOTAL picks, 24 hours before NFL kickoff. Publishing WITHOUT a line (the C-252 no-book-price semantics the brief offers as the alternative) is NOT AVAILABLE: Pick.line is `Float` NOT NULL in schema.prisma, which law 2 freezes. So the only two states are publish-the-mean or refuse, and which one ships is the founder's call. | claude | CLAIMED | Branch claude/launch-line-integrity. Numbered from C-281 deliberately, NOT the next free number on main (C-176): three sibling launch sessions are opening rows tonight and the round2 branch already carries rows to C-263, so a main-relative number would collide on merge. |
| C-282 | LINE INTEGRITY, PART 2 OF 4: automated remediation lane (founder-delegated 2026-09-08, via orchestrator; founder policy since 2026-09-05 is that no pick ever sits on a human). apps/web/lib/settlement/line-integrity-lane.ts, gated LINE_INTEGRITY_VOID_ENABLED default OFF, wired as step 3c of settle-picks after zero-sit and before the outbox drain. VOID half: a SETTLED published SPREAD/TOTAL pick whose stored line was not quoted by any book for that game and market at or before generatedAt is set result=VOID through the same transactional outbox the graders use, one PickSettlementEvent carrying rcaCode LINE_NOT_QUOTED plus storedLine, bookLine (nearest quoted, or null when none) and sourceIds. settledAt is NEVER re-stamped - it is absent from the update `data` and the event carries the ORIGINAL settlement time - and the result is withdrawn, never rewritten in place to another outcome. UNPUBLISH half: an UNSETTLED published pick with the same defect gets isPublished=false in one PENDING-scoped updateMany; nothing is deleted, the result stays PENDING and zero-sit still owns its grading. Idempotent both halves (writes scoped to the state read, so a second run selects nothing and a race loser writes nothing). Nothing is voided on missing evidence: an odds READ FAILURE skips the pick, and NO_QUOTE_ROWS fires only when the query succeeded and returned nothing. MONEYLINE is out of scope (no line). Per-pick write isolation, per-cycle caps 50 void / 200 unpublish. NOTE ON THE BRIEF: it says to read the flag 'exactly the way the zero-sit lane reads its flag' - the zero-sit lane HAS no flag, it ships always-on, so this uses the repo's env-flag idiom instead (trimmed, lower-cased, exact 'true', default false; free-settlement-runner.ts, public-surface-truth/route.ts). Recorded rather than silently substituted. settle-picks keeps the lane's counts SEPARATE from picksVoided/picksUnpublished, which have always meant the zero-sit lane's, and its voids do not clear the starvation check: withdrawing a published result is not grading an overdue pick. | claude | CLAIMED | Branch claude/launch-line-integrity. |

HOW, STATED PRECISELY BECAUSE IT IS NOT THE npm SCRIPT: this session has no DATABASE_URL and no connection-string tool, and law 3 forbids hunting for one, so SQL fetched the tool's INPUTS and the tool's own resolveBookLine/regradeOne plus the engine's calculatePickResult ran over them. Grading logic is the real code path; the Prisma driver is not. Query validated against C-197's published baseline first: on the `line` column it reproduces SPREAD 312 (C-197 said 310, two more settled since), TOTAL 369 exact, MONEYLINE 34 exact. RESULT 1, C-197's SCOPE IS OVERSTATED: it counts off-grid on `line`, but settlement grades against selectGradingLine = clvLockLine ?? line, and on THAT line SPREAD is 186 not 312 and TOTAL 336 not 369 - 523 picks, not ~680. RESULT 2: all 523 had a real book line at publish (NONE=0), and 85 of 523 recorded results would CHANGE under a modal-book-line grade (85 of the 1329 settled published SPREAD/TOTAL overall). Worst bucket is baseball_mlb TOTAL, 242 examined / 58 differ - the sibling-lane gap C-281 identified, no totals twin for the run-line ladder. NFL is 19 off-grid picks with 0 differing. No hit rate derived, per the tool's own rule. RESULT 3 AND IT CORRECTS MY OWN C-281 CLAIM: I wrote flatly that the stored line is a mean of quoted book lines and NOT model output. A mean must lie inside the [min,max] of what it averaged. 442 of 523 do; 81 DO NOT and therefore cannot be a mean of the books quoting at publish (LSU Tigers -35.9 stored while the books present read [-11.5,-10.5], out by 24.42; Mississippi State -48.8 against a book -28.5). soccer_usa_mls (78) and americanfootball_nfl (19) are 100pct in-range; americanfootball_ncaaf SPREAD is 39 in / 35 out. So C-197 was PARTLY RIGHT and C-281's section 1 was PARTLY WRONG, and the doc now says so at the top of that section. WHAT THE 81 ARE IS NOT ESTABLISHED: many show only one or two surviving book lines while MIN_BOOKMAKERS needs two priced books to publish at all, so missing or duplicate-game-keyed odds rows (scripts/ops/merge-duplicate-games.ts exists for that) produce the same signature with no model contamination. Distinguishing them needs the ingestion-run trail for those 81, which nobody has pulled. NOTHING SHIPPED DIFFERENTLY: both flags stay OFF, both explanations are defects, and the guard and lane refuse or withdraw on the same rule either way. No writes of any kind were made to production. | claude | DONE | Measurement recorded in docs/ops/LINE_INTEGRITY_DECISION_2026-09-08.md section 5b on claude/launch-line-integrity, PR #733. | C-275 | DEVIN REVIEW ROUND ON #733 FOUND FIVE REAL DEFECTS IN C-282, AND ONE OF THEM MADE THE WHOLE FEATURE A NO-OP. All five verified against source before touching anything; all five in code I wrote in this same PR. (1) BLOCKING: PickSettlementEvent.pickId is @unique (schema.prisma line 3 of the model) and an ALREADY SETTLED pick necessarily owns that event, written by the grader that settled it - so voidDefectiveSettledPicks' unconditional create violated the constraint, rolled the transaction back, and landed in my own catch as WRITE_FAILED. The VOID half could not void a single pick in production. It passed CI only because my test double's pickSettlementEvent.create accepted anything; the double is now FAITHFUL and throws a unique violation, so the void path can never silently reacquire this. FIX: the withdrawal is recorded as an append-only JarvisMemoryEvent (the same store the UNPUBLISH half uses, distinguished by metadata.action), and the original grading event is left INTACT - which is the better design anyway: the grader's evidence is history and a withdrawal is a new fact about it, not a correction of it. Adding a table or relaxing the constraint is unavailable under law 2. (2) The lane classified `line`, but settlement grades selectGradingLine = clvLockLine ?? line, and `line` is rewritten every refresh cycle while a pick is PENDING - so remediation would withdraw results graded against a good locked line and keep defective ones whose drifted line looks fine. MY OWN C-274 PRODUCTION MEASUREMENT IS THE PROOF: 312 settled SPREAD picks are off-grid on `line`, 186 on the grading line. Fixed in the lane and in the truth-surface screen; evidence now carries gradingLine, storedLine and clvLockLine separately. (3) readQuotesAtPublish matched the stored line against EVERY earlier odds row, including superseded snapshots and non-book writers like rundown_default, so a stale quote could vouch for a line no bookmaker was offering - failing OPEN. Now one snapshot per REAL book at or before generatedAt, using isRealBookmakerKey imported from the canonical apps/web/lib/calibration/publish-time-market-p.ts. NOTE ON MYSELF: my own dry-run tool (C-284) already did this correctly, so I shipped TWO DIVERGENT IMPLEMENTATIONS OF ONE RULE inside a single PR - the exact defect class this repo keeps recording. A drift-guard test now pins the two non-book key lists identical. (4) The lane took no `?sport=` scope while every sibling lane does, so a sport-scoped settle cycle could unpublish and void picks from every other sport; requestedSport is now threaded through the route and both halves. (5) publishedUnsettledOffGridOrBadRunline was DOCUMENTED as an EXACT count while being computed over the first cap rows only - the label-does-not-match-the-measurement defect (C-241/C-246/C-250) inside the very block I wrote to avoid it; it now carries its own Inspected denominator and CapReached flag like every other count. 35 tests (was 25). Each fix negative-controlled independently: reintroducing the settlement-event create fails 8 tests, classifying row.line fails exactly the 2 locked-line tests, returning all odds rows fails exactly the superseded and non-book tests, dropping the sport filter fails exactly the scope test, hardcoding capReached false fails exactly the off-grid cap test. Verify: typecheck 0, lint 0, lint:brand 3775 passed, guardrails 26/26, build exit 0, prediction-engine + ops 1544 passed, apps/web settlement lanes 74 passed. | claude | CLAIMED | Branch claude/launch-line-integrity, PR #733. |
| C-276 | DEVIN REVIEW ROUND 2 ON #733, AND IT IS A LIVENESS BUG THAT WOULD HAVE MADE THE FLIP PRECONDITION UNREACHABLE. Both halves took the OLDEST cap candidates each cycle, where 'candidate' is every published SPREAD/TOTAL pick in the relevant state - not just the defective ones, because 'was this line quoted' needs the odds table and cannot be expressed in a Prisma where. So once the oldest cap rows are CLEAN they are re-selected every cycle forever and a defect behind them is never reached: on production that is 1329 settled published SPREAD/TOTAL picks against a cap of 50. The lane would report inspected=50 acted=0 indefinitely and look healthy, while remainingToVoid - the founder's flip precondition in the decision doc - could never reach 0. FIX: a durable sweep cursor per half (the last pick id inspected) appended to JarvisMemoryEvent under its OWN scope settlement.line-integrity.cursor, so the ops action counts that filter on metadata.action can never pick a cursor up; ordering moved from generatedAt to id so the cursor is stable; each cycle resumes after it and RESETS on exhaustion so the next sweep wraps. Wrapping is required rather than one-and-done because a pick generated long ago can settle later and land behind a cursor that already passed it; re-inspecting a clean row costs one odds read and changes nothing, so it is safe. schema.prisma is frozen (law 2) so no new table or column - the existing append-only memory table carries it. 38 tests (was 35), including the multi-cycle case the reviewer asked for: two clean picks ahead of one defect with cap 2, where cycle one acts on nothing and cycle two voids the defect. Negative control: deleting the cursor clause fails exactly the three progress tests. ALSO CORRECTED IN THIS ROW: my first version of that wrap test asserted `third.pickQueriesHadCursor ?? false` - a field that does not exist, so it read undefined ?? false and passed vacuously; it now asserts the third sweep's query carries no cursor. A test that cannot fail is not evidence. | C-277 | DEVIN ROUNDS 2-3 ON #733: THREE REDS AND FOUR YELLOWS, ALL VERIFIED IN SOURCE, ALL FIXED. Stakes are higher than the earlier rounds because the coordinator says LINE_INTEGRITY_VOID_ENABLED=true is flipped tonight, so the lane must be right on the FIRST cycle. (R1, the one that mattered most) THE LANE HAD NO TIME BUDGET. It runs at settle-picks step 3c, AFTER zero-sit and BEFORE the slate freeze and outbox drain, and a full page is up to 250 SEQUENTIAL odds reads (50 void + 200 unpublish) - so it could consume the route's 300s maxDuration and starve the settlement work it exists to protect. A void lane that starves settlement breaks the thing it protects. Both halves now take deadlineAtMs + clock and stop before EVERY odds read and EVERY write, counting the rest as DEADLINE_REACHED; the route passes lineIntegrityDeadline(startedAt, maxDuration), the same absolute deadline zero-sit uses, so the two lanes share ONE 60s tail reserve rather than each carving their own. (R2) LEGACY ROWS WITH NO clvLockLine were judged on a REFRESHED `line` against quotes capped at generatedAt - comparing a possibly post-publish value against pre-publish odds, which can void a legitimately quoted pick. Such a row is now SKIPPED as NO_PUBLISH_LOCK unless the immutable PickProofReceipt rescues it (its own `line` at its own `asOf`); the lane refuses to guess. (R3) SETTLED AND PENDING ASK DIFFERENT QUESTIONS and the lane asked one. Settled = 'what did we grade': the lock, against the board at generatedAt. Pending = 'what are we showing': the DISPLAYED `line`, against the CURRENT board - judging the lock there leaves a presently unplaceable published pick up because its publish-time lock was fine, the opposite of the point. judgementFor() names the line, the clock and the basis, and evidence now cites judgedLine + judgedBasis instead of a value the decision did not use (that also closes the yellow about unpublish evidence citing the wrong line). (Y1) THE PUBLISH GUARD ACCEPTED A rundown_default LINE AS A QUOTE. NON_BOOK_BOOKMAKER_KEYS/isRealBookmakerKey now live in the ENGINE as the canonical source - it needs them and cannot import from apps/web - and apps/web/lib/calibration/publish-time-market-p.ts re-exports rather than keeping a second copy; both scorers filter guard input to real books. (Y3) THE SWEEP CURSOR WAS GLOBAL, so a sport-scoped run advanced it and skipped rows the unscoped sweep had never seen; the key is now half:sportKey. (Y4) WITHDRAWAL COULD NOT REOPEN COMPLETED POST-SETTLEMENT WORK: enqueuePostSettlementWork is createMany+skipDuplicates on unique (subjectId,kind), so on a settled pick whose rows are already DONE it is a NO-OP and the CLV grade and signal snapshot keep the outcome just withdrawn. New reopenPostSettlementWork() sets them back to PENDING and clears completedAt/lastError; no reopenedAt column exists and schema.prisma is frozen, so status carries it and attemptCount preserves history. 49 lane tests (was 38) + 18 engine. EVERY fix red-checked; ONE CORRECTION WORTH RECORDING: my first Y1 test PASSED with the fix reverted because the fixture's non-book value was not actually the mean (mean of 5 real books, not an invented decimal), so the guard refused for the wrong reason - the fixture was recomputed and the control now fails exactly the two non-book tests. A test that cannot fail is not evidence, and that is the second time this session; I now compute fixture arithmetic instead of eyeballing it. | C-278 | DEVIN ROUND 4, THE LAST RED, AND MY OWN C-277 FIX CREATED THE LIVE PATH. A withdrawn pick could keep a PUBLIC CLV verdict. Three links verified in source: (1) C-277 reopened CLV_GRADE on void; (2) drainPendingClvGrades (apps/web/lib/settlement/free-path-clv.ts) selects `result: { not: PENDING }`, which INCLUDES VOID, so the reopened row drains and a fresh clvVerdict is written; (3) loadPublicClvPolicy (apps/web/lib/performance/public-clv-policy.ts) counts on isBootstrap:false + isPublished:true + clvVerdict not null with NO result filter, so that verdict lands in the public sample. FIX, three places that must agree: the void lane enqueues and reopens SNAPSHOT_OUTCOME ONLY; drainPendingClvGrades excludes VOID as well as PENDING (defence in depth, so any other enqueuer cannot grade one either); loadPublicClvPolicy excludes VOID from all four counts. | C-280 | ROUND 7: MY OWN C-278 VOID EXCLUSION STARVED THE CLV DRAIN, AND IT IS THE SAME CLASS AS C-276. drainPendingClvGrades selects PENDING CLV_GRADE work oldest-first with a fixed take, then C-278 filtered VOID subjects out of the pick query - so a withdrawn pick still CONSUMED a take slot, was dropped from the batch, and because nothing retired its work row it was re-selected on every subsequent cycle. Enough withdrawn picks at the head of the oldest-first queue and no valid repair behind them ever runs. Verified in source before changing anything; the snapshot drain does NOT have this shape because it treats VOID as a real result and processes it. EXCLUDING WORK WITHOUT RETIRING IT CONVERTS A CORRECTNESS FIX INTO A LIVENESS BUG - identical in shape to C-276, where clean rows held the oldest page forever, and I did not carry that lesson across when I wrote C-278. FIX: the drain no longer filters on result at all. It loads the selected subjects WITH their result and partitions in code - WIN/LOSS/PUSH grade, VOID is RETIRED, PENDING is left alone because it is legitimately still waiting. Retirement is a new terminal status CANCELLED via cancelPostSettlementWork(), distinct from DONE (the work happened) and FAILED (retry it): a CLV grade owed on a withdrawn pick is neither, and there is no repair that would change that. Safe without a schema change because PostSettlementWork.status is a plain String, not an enum, and the only two readers filter on status PENDING (the CLV and snapshot drains), so a CANCELLED row is simply invisible to them - which is the point. Nothing is deleted, the reason is stored in lastError, and the stored clvVerdict is untouched. Withdrawn work is retired FIRST so an all-VOID batch frees its slots in the same cycle. 5 new drain tests including the two the coordinator asked for (an all-VOID oldest batch no longer blocks the next; a mixed batch grades the valid picks and retires the withdrawn work) plus a still-PENDING pick is left in the queue. The test double now models the real queue - work rows carry a status and only leave when something writes a terminal one - because the previous double returned a fixed list and could not have shown starvation at all. TWO PROCESS NOTES: my first assertion on the all-VOID test checked pick.update calls, which are absent on the no-close path, so it asserted the wrong artifact and I corrected it to the real liveness property; and a careless string replace edited an unrelated pre-existing cast at line 75 which I reverted rather than leave in the diff. Red-checked: skipping the retire fails exactly 3 tests, restoring the SQL filter fails exactly 1. | C-293 | (authored as C-274 on PR #739; renumbered here, ID collision with main) THE POOLED ECE THE GATE READS IS 44% CANCELLATION, AND THE DECOMPOSITION THAT PROVES IT HAD NEVER BEEN RUN. AGENTS.md records the open question verbatim: expectedCalibrationError stores weighted ABSOLUTE per-bin gaps, so pooled sitting below every stratum 'is a plausible mechanism, not an observed one, and proving it needs an aligned per-bin decomposition nobody has run'. Ran it. Because production bins are FIXED equal-width intervals, bin k is the same interval for every stratum, which makes this an EXACT identity rather than an approximation: SUM_s w_s*ECE_s - ECE_pooled equals SUM_k (n_k/N) * [SUM_s w_s,k*abs(d_s,k) - abs(SUM_s w_s,k*d_s,k)], every bracketed term non-negative by the triangle inequality. So the gap between pooled and the weighted stratum mean is ENTIRELY signed-error cancellation. On the four figures recorded in AGENTS.md (which sum to exactly the recorded n=458, confirming the disjoint+exhaustive precondition the identity needs): pooled 0.0524, honest weighted mean 0.0938, cancellation 0.0414 = 44.2%. The honest figure is 1.88x the 0.05 floor and the DEPLOYED v5.2.7 measures 0.1089 on its own 245 rows, 2.18x the floor. PROVENANCE STATED PLAINLY IN FINDINGS.md: those four inputs are QUOTED from this repo's own record, not measured - this session has no DB access and law 7 forbids acquiring any. What is new is the arithmetic, not the data. CONSEQUENCE: 'wait for more rows' is dead as a strategy - as v5.2.7's weight rises toward 1 the honest figure converges UP toward 0.1089 (measured path 0.0938, 0.0965, 0.0991, 0.1017, 0.1042, 0.1069 at plus 0/100/250/500/1000/3000 rows). Tooling is a new top-level gse-calibration-lab/, additions only, no existing repo file modified. Metrics are a PARITY-PROVEN port of the production TypeScript - parity/*.mjs transpiles the real ece.ts, brier.ts, probability-calibration.ts and calibration-eligibility.ts with the repo's own esbuild and asserts agreement to 1e-12 across 15 fixtures x 3 bin counts plus 12 gate cases with exact reason strings. Core is pure stdlib so it runs with NO pip at all (law 7 forbids installing). Cannot flip a gate: floors looser than production defaults raise. Cannot invent data: no demo mode, and a stratum column absent from every row raises, because a single-stratum run reports cancellation 0.0000 which is indistinguishable from a real finding of none. | claude | DONE | PR #739. Two candidate designs: (a) a durable per-CUSTOMER exclusion (one open commercial intent at a time, superseding rather than paralleling), or (b) reconcile-and-reuse - before minting, list the customer's OPEN subscription-mode Checkout Sessions and either return the existing URL or expire it. HONEST RESIDUAL, not closed and not claimed closed: two requests that BOTH list before EITHER has created a session still both see none. That window is microseconds instead of the whole checkout flow and any pair separated by one round trip converges, but it is a narrowing, not atomicity. Full serialisation needs a durable per-CUSTOMER exclusion - a unique index the schema does not have and law 2 forbids an agent adding - and the alternative, holding a Postgres advisory lock across the Stripe network call, trades a rare double-charge for routine pinned connections and blown transaction timeouts. | C-278a | RUNDOWN THIN-FILL THAT SURVIVES THE DAY, PLUS THE KALSHI TAKER FEE. Production logs every refresh cycle as `rundown empty (2d): HTTP 429 rate_limited` for all four in-season sports: TheRundown is the only registered commercial-use second book and our own cadence (refresh-odds every 15 min plus board-fill 4x/h, 4 sports, 2 dates, no back-off) burns the free 20k/day quota by morning, so the feed is gone through NFL Week 1 kickoff - which is exactly when the truth surface reads MONEYLINE 0 and TOTAL 0 on the two NFL games in the 72h window, because ESPN is one book and MIN_BOOKMAKERS is 2. RATIONING, five conditions in shouldThinFillFromRundown(): a key exists; no 429 cooldown open for the sport; the sport is NFL or NCAAF; at least one game sits UNDER MIN_BOOKMAKERS (a covered slate is never dual-pulled); at least one of those thin games is inside the 72h board window (6h in-play grace, unparseable kickoff refused). One call per SPORT per cycle, never per game. A 429 now surfaces as a STRUCTURED rd.rateLimited rather than a string match on the error text, and opens a 30-minute in-process cooldown for that sport covering BOTH the thin-fill and full-replace legs; one log line with the resume instant, never a key. KALSHI TAKER FEE: Rundown affiliate 25 maps to book key `kalshi`, which PASSES isRealBookmakerKey (NON_BOOK_BOOKMAKER_KEYS holds only `rundown_default`, packages/prediction-engine/src/scoring.ts:1017) so no allow-list change was needed. But a Kalshi ask is an EXCHANGE price: no book margin, and a taker fee on top, so the raw ask understates what a member pays and de-vig would report an edge nobody could take. fees = ceil(0.07 * C * P * (1-P)) rounded UP to the next cent, effective P + fee/C, applied at the one place the affiliate is known. ONE CORRECTION WORTH RECORDING: my first ceil was raw, and 0.07*100*0.6*0.4*100 evaluates to 168.00000000000003 in binary floating point - it would have charged 169c on an order whose exact fee is 168c, an invented cost. Snapped to 9dp before the ceil and the value-3 test pins it. Every gate red-checked and each fails exactly its own test: sport gate removed fails 1, window gate 1, cooldown gate 1, un-recorded 429 fails 1, a never-lapsing cooldown fails 3, un-wired fee fails 1. | C-299 | NEVER GENERATE OR RE-SCORE A PICK AFTER KICKOFF - THE SECOND CLOCK, AND IT IS OURS. Reported: 113 of 477 settled moneyline picks carry generatedAt >= games.commenceTime; one, Minnesota Twins ML (-1771), was generated 02:03Z on a 01:40Z first pitch off in-play quotes (-1000 to -3335) and froze a proof receipt at marketFairProb 0.884 on a game the Padres came back and won. WHAT I VERIFIED IN SOURCE RATHER THAN ASSUMED, because the picture is not the one the finding implies: both paths ALREADY refuse an in-play game, via the C-111 fixture guard (fixture-confirmation.ts:290, status event_already_started) which is fail-closed on a failed board fetch. That guard reads exactly ONE clock, the ESPN scoreboard's listed kickoff, and never compares our own commenceTime to now. The residual is the two clocks disagreeing: a provisional or stale listing whose kickoff still reads ahead, or a same-teams match onto a later event, confirms a game that by our row has started. That is the hole this closes, and it is narrower than 113 rows - most of those predate the C-111 guard, and I did NOT measure how many came through the residual, so I am not claiming it. NEW packages/ingestion-pipeline/src/in-play-guard.ts, one rule both paths share: hasKickedOff(kickoff, now) is exactly kickoff <= now with NO pre-kickoff cushion (a game 5 minutes out is still a legitimate pre-game claim) and fails CLOSED on an unparseable clock. It takes the EFFECTIVE kickoff, not the raw row value, because commenceTimeCorrection exists precisely because a stale row can carry a wrong clock ESPN corrects - refusing on the raw value would have suppressed legitimate games, a regression I nearly shipped. process-sport.ts: the check sits before confirmedGameIds.add, so an in-play game gets no OddsInput, is never scored, and the ONE membership test at the write refuses its create, its PENDING refresh of selection/line/confidence/factorBreakdown, and its receipt mint. generate-signal-slate.ts: same rule, but honestly labelled DEFENCE IN DEPTH - the scanned-games query already filters commenceTime gte now, so this is not a live hole there. RECEIPTS: minted once, process-sport.ts:1387-1403, upsert with update:{} - correct as-is, no fix needed; the real exposure was that the FIRST mint could happen in-play, which the generation gate now prevents. skippedInPlay counted and reported on both envelopes and both log lines. CORRECTION I HAVE TO RECORD, AND IT IS THE SAME VACUOUS-TEST CLASS THE C-277..C-280 NOTES WARN ABOUT: I first added a second kickoff check AT the write as defence in depth, and its red-check changed NOTHING - the game-loop guard already keeps the id out of confirmedGameIds, so it was dead code implying a hole that does not exist. I REMOVED it and pinned the real protection instead; removing the confirmedGameIds gate now fails 7 tests across C-111 and C-299. Also relativised the whole process-sport suite off a fixed 2026-06-12 anchor that had rotted into the past, where the new guard would have read every fixture as in-play. CONSEQUENCE: when the performance gate opens, the summary section renders its empty state, not a record - the page cannot show data at all however long it runs. NOT FIXED HERE ON PURPOSE: the fix is a WRITE path into a table a public page reads, and landing that unattended overnight is the shape of the mistakes this ledger exists to prevent.
